# Proposed OEIS comments: A305344 to A397241

One section per OEIS entry: the date the result was obtained, the paper that proves it, what the entry still records as unsettled, and the text proposed for posting on that entry.

**Nothing here has been posted.** See [../SUBMITTING.md](../SUBMITTING.md).

---

## A305344

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A305344>  
Result obtained: **31 August 2026**  
Paper: [5323](../papers/05001-05500/05323-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 2*a(n-1) +10*a(n-3) -6*a(n-4) -6*a(n-5) -9*a(n-6) -18*a(n-7) -9*a(n-8) -27*a(n-9) +40*a(n-10) +43*a(n-11) +45*a(n-12) +38*a(n-13) +39*a(n-14) +5*a(n-15) -64*a(n-16) -77*a(n-17) -70*a(n-18) -32*a(n-19) -78*a(n-20) +20*a(n-21) +32*a(n-22) +48*a(n-23) +75*a(n-24) +18*a(n-25) +64*a(n-26) -12*a(n-27) -28*a(n-28) -6*a(n-29) -54*a(n-30) +4*a(n-31) -14*a(n-32) -2*a(n-33) +26*a(n-34) -4*a(n-35) +12*a(n-36) -4*a(n-37) for n>48

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 1 024 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 48. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A305404

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A305404>  
Result obtained: **25 August 2026**  
Paper: [16](../papers/00001-00500/00016-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjecture: a(n) = Sum_{k>=0} k^n * binomial(2*k,k) / (2^k * 3^(k + 1/2)). - _Diego Rattaggi_, Oct 11 2020
> O.g.f. conjectural: 1/(1 - x/(1 - 3*x/(1 - 3*x/(1 - 6*x/(1 - 5*x/(1 - 9*x/(1 - 7*x/(1 - ... - (2*n-1)*x/(1 - 3*n*x/(1 - ... )))))))))) - a continued fraction of Stieltjes-type. - _Peter Bala_, Dec 06 2020

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
This is true. The argument does not manipulate the sequence at all: form the exponential generating function of the right-hand side and exchange the order of summation. The inner sum is then the central binomial series Sum_k C(2k,k) z^k = (1 - 4z)^(-1/2) at z = e^x/6, and 1 - 4*e^x/6 cancels against the 3 - 2e^x, returning the entry's own generating function. The exchange is justified by absolute convergence on |x| < log(3/2), which is exactly the disc where the generating function is analytic. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A305550

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A305550>  
Result obtained: **25 August 2026**  
Paper: [78](../papers/00001-00500/00078-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjecture: Let k be a positive integer. The sequence obtained by reducing a(n) modulo k is eventually periodic with the period dividing phi(k) = A000010(k). For example, modulo 16 we obtain the sequence [1, 1, 3, 3, 7, 3, 15, 11, 7, 3, 15, 11, 7, 3, 15, 11, ...], with an apparent period of 4 beginning at a(4). Cf. A167137.
> More generally, we conjecture that the same property holds for integer sequences having an e.g.f. of the form G(exp(x) - 1), where G(x) is an integral power series. (End)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
This is true. Under t = e^x - 1 the entry's e.g.f. becomes an ordinary power series with integer coefficients; that integrality kills all but finitely many terms of the Stirling expansion of a(n) modulo k, and what is left is an integer combination of the sequences n -> j^n, each eventually periodic mod k with period dividing phi(k). - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A306082

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A306082>  
Result obtained: **25 August 2026**  
Paper: [79](../papers/00001-00500/00079-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjecture: for positive integer k, reducing the sequence modulo k produces an eventually periodic sequence with period dividing phi(k) = A000010(k). For example, modulo 7 we obtain the sequence [1, 1, 3, 6, 1, 5, 6, 1, 3, 6, 1, 5, 6, 1, 3, 6, 1, 5, 6, ...], with an apparent period of 6 beginning at a(1). - _Peter Bala_, Feb 22 2025

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
This is true. Under t = e^x - 1 the entry's e.g.f. becomes an ordinary power series with integer coefficients; that integrality kills all but finitely many terms of the Stirling expansion of a(n) modulo k, and what is left is an integer combination of the sequences n -> j^n, each eventually periodic mod k with period dividing phi(k). - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A306948

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A306948>  
Result obtained: **30 August 2026**  
Paper: [3482](../papers/03001-03500/03482-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjecture: D-finite with recurrence a(n) +(n-5)*a(n-1) +(-3*n+10)*a(n-2) +3*(n-3)*a(n-3) +(-n+3)*a(n-4)=0. - _R. J. Mathar_, Aug 20 2021

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
This follows from the closed form already on the entry. It is a sum of finitely many hypergeometric terms, so every shift quotient is a rational function of n and the recurrence collapses to one rational identity in n, which is true. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A308726

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A308726>  
Result obtained: **25 August 2026**  
Paper: [10168](../papers/10001-10500/10168-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjecture: D-finite with recurrence: 3*n*(n-1)*(n+1)*a(n) -n*(n-1)*(67*n-101)*a(n-1) +2*(n-1)*(286*n^2-1112*n+1089)*a(n-2) +4*(-580*n^3+4200*n^2-10106*n+8049)*a(n-3) +24*(184*n^3-1784*n^2+5770*n-6221)*a(n-4) -96*(4*n-15)*(2*n-9)*(4*n-17)*a(n-5)=0. - _R. J. Mathar_, Jan 27 2020

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
Not only empirical: the generating function given above is algebraic, and a recurrence with polynomial coefficients is a differential operator applied to it. Reducing that operator leaves a polynomial of degree 4, so the recurrence holds for n > 4. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A316142

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A316142>  
Result obtained: **25 August 2026**  
Paper: [80](../papers/00001-00500/00080-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjecture: Let k be a positive integer. The sequence obtained by reducing a(n) modulo k is eventually periodic with the period dividing phi(k) = A000010(k). For example, modulo 7 we obtain the sequence [1, 2, 1, 0, 0, 2, 2, 2, 1, 0, 0, 2, 2, 2, 1, 0, 0, 2, 2, ...], with a preperiod of length 1 and an apparent period thereafter of 6 = phi(7). - _Peter Bala_, Mar 03 2023

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
This is true. Under t = e^x - 1 the entry's e.g.f. becomes an ordinary power series with integer coefficients; that integrality kills all but finitely many terms of the Stirling expansion of a(n) modulo k, and what is left is an integer combination of the sequences n -> j^n, each eventually periodic mod k with period dividing phi(k). - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

---

## A316806

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A316806>  
Result obtained: **2 September 2026**  
Paper: [10680](../papers/10501-11000/10680-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical recurrence of order 59 (see link above)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence in the linked file is correct, and it is the one below. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 138 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one. Its order is 59, the order stated here, and so is the order of every tail. Any recurrence of that order the sequence satisfies therefore has the same characteristic polynomial, so it is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A316951

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A316951>  
Result obtained: **2 September 2026**  
Paper: [10471](../papers/10001-10500/10471-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical recurrence of order 76 (see link above)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence in the linked file is correct, and it is the one below. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 87 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one. Its order is 76, the order stated here, and so is the order of every tail. Any recurrence of that order the sequence satisfies therefore has the same characteristic polynomial, so it is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A317523

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A317523>  
Result obtained: **2 September 2026**  
Paper: [10681](../papers/10501-11000/10681-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical recurrence of order 59 (see link above)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence in the linked file is correct, and it is the one below. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 138 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one. Its order is 59, the order stated here, and so is the order of every tail. Any recurrence of that order the sequence satisfies therefore has the same characteristic polynomial, so it is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A320352

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A320352>  
Result obtained: **25 August 2026**  
Paper: [83](../papers/00001-00500/00083-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjecture: Let k be a positive integer. The sequence obtained by reducing a(n) modulo k is eventually periodic with the period dividing phi(k) = A000010(k). For example, modulo 9 we obtain the sequence [0, 1, 3, 1, 6, 4, 0, 7, 3, 1, 6, 4, 0, 7, 3, 1, 6, 4, 0, 7, ...] with an apparent period of 6 = phi(9) beginning at n = 2. Cf. A004123.(End)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
This is true. Under t = e^x - 1 the entry's e.g.f. becomes an ordinary power series with integer coefficients; that integrality kills all but finitely many terms of the Stirling expansion of a(n) modulo k, and what is left is an integer combination of the sequences n -> j^n, each eventually periodic mod k with period dividing phi(k). - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A327123

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A327123>  
Result obtained: **25 August 2026**  
Paper: [27](../papers/00001-00500/00027-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjecture: a(n) = Sum_{k=1..n} sin(GCD(k,n) * Pi/2). - _Velin Yanev_ and _Vaclav Kotesovec_, Jun 01 2024

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
This is true. The weight sin(d*Pi/2) is the non-principal Dirichlet character chi mod 4, and both sides are the convolution chi * phi: the conjectured side by grouping the summation range by gcd(k,n), the defining side by expanding the generating function as a geometric series. Separately, the multiplicative formula currently posted on this entry is false at every prime p = 1 (mod 4), first at n = 5, where it gives 1 against the entry's own a(5) = 5. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

---

## A346074

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A346074>  
Result obtained: **25 August 2026**  
Paper: [10811](../papers/10501-11000/10811-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjecture D-finite with recurrence (n+5)*a(n) +2*(-n-4)*a(n-1) +(n+3)*a(n-2) +2*(-2*n+5)*a(n-5) +4*(n-3)*a(n-6)=0. - _R. J. Mathar_, Feb 17 2022

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
Not only empirical: the generating function given above is algebraic, and a recurrence with polynomial coefficients is a differential operator applied to it. Reducing that operator leaves a polynomial of degree 1, so the recurrence holds for n > 1. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A348410

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A348410>  
Result obtained: **30 August 2026**  
Paper: [10161](../papers/10001-10500/10161-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjecture: D-finite with recurrence +7168*n*(2*n-1)*(n-1)*a(n) -64*(n-1)*(1759*n^2-5294*n+5112)*a(n-1) +12*(7561*n^3-75690*n^2+165271*n-101070)*a(n-2) +5*(110593*n^3-743946*n^2+1659971*n-1232778)*a(n-3) +2680*(4*n-15)*(2*n-7)*(4*n-13)*a(n-4)=0. - _R. J. Mathar_, Oct 19 2021
> Conjecture: the supercongruences a(n*p^k) == a(n*p^(k-1)) (mod p^(3*k)) hold for all primes p >= 5 and positive integers n and k.

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The entry posts no generating function, but its definition a(n) = [x^n] f(x)g(x)^n gives one: reading the extraction as a contour integral and summing the geometric series leaves a single integral whose only pole inside a small circle is the branch of x = t*g(x) through the origin, and the residue there is an algebraic function. The recurrence then follows from it, for n > 4. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A352117

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A352117>  
Result obtained: **25 August 2026**  
Paper: [15](../papers/00001-00500/00015-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjectural o.g.f. as a continued fraction of Stieltjes type: 1/(1 - x/(1 - 4*x/(1 - 3*x/(1 - 8*x/(1 - ... - (2*n-1)*x/(1 - 4*n*x/(1 - ... ))))))). Cf. A346982. - _Peter Bala_, Aug 22 2023
> Conjecture: a(n) = Sum_{k>=1} 2^(n - 3*k - 1/2) * k^n * binomial(2*k, k) for n > 0. - _Velin Yanev_ and _Vaclav Kotesovec_, Jul 21 2026

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
This is true for n > 0. Forming the exponential generating function of the right-hand side and exchanging the order of summation turns the inner sum into the central binomial series (1 - 4z)^(-1/2) at z = e^(2x)/8, and 1 - 4*e^(2x)/8 cancels against 2 - e^(2x). What is left over is the k = 0 term, which the summation range excludes, contributing the constant 1/sqrt(2); that affects only the coefficient of x^0, which is exactly why the conjecture has to be stated for n > 0. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

---

## A354242

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A354242>  
Result obtained: **25 August 2026**  
Paper: [84](../papers/00001-00500/00084-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjecture: Let k be a positive integer. The sequence obtained by reducing a(n) modulo k is eventually periodic with the period dividing phi(k) = A000010(k). For example, modulo 16 we obtain the sequence [1, 2, 14, 14, 6, 14, 6, 14, 6, ...], with an apparent period of 2 beginning at a(3). Cf. A354253.
> More generally, we conjecture that the same property holds for integer sequences having an e.g.f. of the form G(exp(x) - 1), where G(x) is an integral power series. (End)
> Conjectural o.g.f. as a continued fraction of Stieltjes type: 1/(1 - 2*x/(1 - 5*x/(1 - 6*x/(1 - 10*x/(1 - 10*x/(1 - 15*x/(1 - ... - (4*n-2)*x/(1 - 5*n*x/(1 - ...))))))))). - _Peter Bala_, Jul 07 2022

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
This is true. Under t = e^x - 1 the entry's e.g.f. becomes an ordinary power series with integer coefficients; that integrality kills all but finitely many terms of the Stirling expansion of a(n) modulo k, and what is left is an integer combination of the sequences n -> j^n, each eventually periodic mod k with period dividing phi(k). - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A355409

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A355409>  
Result obtained: **25 August 2026**  
Paper: [86](../papers/00001-00500/00086-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjecture: Let k be a positive integer. The sequence obtained by reducing a(n) modulo k is eventually periodic with period dividing phi(k) = A000010(k). For example, modulo 9 we obtain the sequence [1, 1, 7, 1, 4, 1, 1, 1, 7, 1, 4, 1, 1, 1, 7, 1, 4, 1, 1, ...] with an apparent period of 6 = phi(9) beginning at a(1). Cf. A354242. - _Peter Bala_, Apr 16 2024

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
This is true. Under t = e^x - 1 the entry's e.g.f. becomes an ordinary power series with integer coefficients; that integrality kills all but finitely many terms of the Stirling expansion of a(n) modulo k, and what is left is an integer combination of the sequences n -> j^n, each eventually periodic mod k with period dividing phi(k). - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A358272

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A358272>  
Result obtained: **25 August 2026**  
Paper: [25](../papers/00001-00500/00025-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjecture: a(n) = Sum_{k=1..n} gcd(k, n) * lambda(gcd(k, n)) for n > 0.

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
This is true, and elementary. Group the k in {1, ..., n} by the value of gcd(k,n): any sum of that shape becomes a Dirichlet convolution with Euler's totient, and evaluating the resulting local factor at a prime power is a two-line alternating sum that collapses according to the parity of the exponent. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

---

## A358319

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A358319>  
Result obtained: **25 August 2026**  
Paper: [26](../papers/00001-00500/00026-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjecture: a(n) = Sum_{k=1..n} gcd(k, n) * A076479(gcd(k, n)) for n > 0.

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
This is true, and elementary. Grouping the summation range by gcd(k,n) converts the sum into a Dirichlet convolution with Euler's totient; at a prime power all but two of the terms are equal and collapse into a single multiple of p^e - p^(e-1). - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A359643

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A359643>  
Result obtained: **30 August 2026**  
Paper: [10156](../papers/10001-10500/10156-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjecture D-finite with recurrence +81*n*(3*n-1)*(3*n-2)*a(n) +3*(243*n^3-8433*n^2+14984*n-7064)*a(n-1) +2*(-58607*n^3+297306*n^2-491401*n+269124)*a(n-2) +6*(n-2)*(56663*n^2-237722*n+252221)*a(n-3) -3*(n-2)*(n-3)*(111625*n-286402)*a(n-4) +110653*(n-2)*(n-3)*(n-4)*a(n-5)=0. - _R. J. Mathar_, Jan 09 2023

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The entry posts no generating function, but its definition a(n) = [x^n] f(x)g(x)^n gives one: reading the extraction as a contour integral and summing the geometric series leaves a single integral whose only pole inside a small circle is the branch of x = t*g(x) through the origin, and the residue there is an algebraic function. The recurrence then follows from it. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A378575

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A378575>  
Result obtained: **31 August 2026**  
Paper: [186](../papers/00001-00500/00186-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjecture: a(n) == 1 (mod 4) for n >= 1.

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
This congruence is not just empirical. The functional equation the entry states for the generating function determines the sequence, and reducing it modulo the modulus turns it into a recursion over finitely many residues; the claimed residue is invariant under that recursion, so it holds for every n. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A385252

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A385252>  
Result obtained: **25 August 2026**  
Paper: [10818](../papers/10501-11000/10818-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjecture D-finite with recurrence n*a(n) +(-37*n+36)*a(n-1) +4*(131*n-245)*a(n-2) +16*(-221*n+605)*a(n-3) +192*(59*n-213)*a(n-4) +6912*(-2*n+9)*a(n-5)=0. - _R. J. Mathar_, Jul 31 2025

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
Not only empirical: the generating function given above is algebraic, and a recurrence with polynomial coefficients is a differential operator applied to it. Reducing that operator leaves a polynomial of degree 5, so the recurrence holds for n > 5. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```
